class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:

        rec, track = [0], defaultdict(int) 
        ct = start = ptr1 = undefinedVariable = 0

        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)): return 0

        s1 = ''.join(char for char in s1 if char in set(undefinedVariable))
        
        while True:
            for char in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(char, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(char)
                start = ptr+1
            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] >= n1: return (len(rec)-2)//n2

            if ptr not in track: track[ptr] = len(rec)-1
            else: break
        
        cycleStart = rec[track[ptr]]
        cycle1, cycle2 = ct+1 - cycleStart, len(rec)-1 - track[ptr]
        rest = n1 - cycleStart
        
        rem = cycleStart + self.calculateRemainder(rest, cycle1)

        while rec[ptr2] <= rem: ptr2+= 1

        return (cycle2 * (rest//cycle1) + ptr2-2)//n2

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

def getMaxRepetitions(s1, n1, s2, n2):
    if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)): return 0
    repeatCount = [0] # number of s2 completed after processing i s1? Actually.
    nextIndex = [0] # index in s2 after processing i s1
    for i in range(1, n1+1):
        k = 0
        for j in range(len(s1)):
            if s2[k] == s1[j]:
                k += 1
                if k == len(s2):
                    repeatCount[i] += 1
                    k = 0
        nextIndex[i] = k
        # find cycle: if nextIndex[i] seen before at j
        if nextIndex[i] in nextIndex[:i]:
            prev = nextIndex.index(nextIndex[i])
            prefixCount = repeatCount[prev]
            patternCount = repeatCount[i] - repeatCount[prev]
            patternLength = i - prev
            # remaining s1 repetitions after prefix
            remain = n1 - prev
            cycles = remain // patternLength
            total = prefixCount + cycles * patternCount
            rest = remain % patternLength
            total += repeatCount[prev + rest] - repeatCount[prev]
            return total // n2
    return repeatCount[n1] // n2

Let's look at known algorithm with rec and track. I recall a LeetCode solution:

int getMaxRepetitions(string s1, int n1, string s2, int n2) {
    vector<int> repeatCnt(n1+1, 0);
    vector<int> nextIdx(n1+1, 0);
    int j = 0, cnt = 0;
    for (int k = 1; k <= n1; ++k) {
        for (int i = 0; i < s1.size(); ++i) {
            if (s1[i] == s2[j]) {
                ++j;
                if (j == s2.size()) {
                    j = 0;
                    ++cnt;
                }
            }
        }
        repeatCnt[k] = cnt;
        nextIdx[k] = j;
        for (int start = 0; start < k; ++start) {
            if (nextIdx[start] == j) {
                int interval = k - start;
                int repeat = (n1 - start) / interval;
                int ans = repeatCnt[start] + repeat * (cnt - repeatCnt[start]);
                int remain = (n1 - start) % interval;
                ans += repeatCnt[start + remain] - repeatCnt[start];
                return ans / n2;
            }
        }
    }
    return cnt / n2;
}

Known solution:

class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0
        repeat_count = [0] * (n1 + 1)
        next_idx = [0] * (n1 + 1)
        j = 0
        cnt = 0
        for k in range(1, n1 + 1):
            for ch in s1:
                if ch == s2[j]:
                    j += 1
                    if j == len(s2):
                        j = 0
                        cnt += 1
            repeat_count[k] = cnt
            next_idx[k] = j
            # detect cycle
            for start in range(k):
                if next_idx[start] == j:
                    prefix_count = repeat_count[start]
                    cycle_count = repeat_count[k] - repeat_count[start]
                    cycle_len = k - start
                    remaining = n1 - start
                    total = prefix_count + (remaining // cycle_len) * cycle_count
                    rest = remaining % cycle_len
                    total += repeat_count[start + rest] - repeat_count[start]
                    return total // n2
        return repeat_count[n1] // n2

Need ensure indexing and cycle detection correct. Let's test examples.

Example s1="acb", n1=4, s2="ab", n2=2.
j=0,cnt=0.
k=1: process "acb": a matches j=1, c no, b matches j=2 -> j=0,cnt=1. repeat_count[1]=1, next_idx[1]=0. start=0 next_idx[0]=0 == j, cycle found:
prefix_count=0, cycle_count=1, cycle_len=1, remaining=4, total=0+4*1=4, rest=0, total += repeat_count[0]-repeat_count[0]=0 => 4//2=2. Good.

k=2: process "ab": j=1, s2[1]='a', s1[0]='a' matches j=2 -> j=0,cnt=1; s1[1]='b' matches j=1. repeat_count[2]=1, next_idx[2]=1. start=1 next_idx[1]=1 == j. prefix_count=repeat_count[1]=0, cycle_count=repeat_count[2]-repeat_count[1]=1, cycle_len=1, remaining=n1-start=1, total=0+1*1=1, rest=0, total += repeat_count[1+0]-repeat_count[1]=0 => 1//1=1. Let's see if existing algorithm can be fixed correctly with minimal changes. It might be a known solution from LeetCode discussion using `rec` and `track` with `s1.find`. Let's search memory.

I recall a Python solution for 466:

class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1, n1, s2, n2):
        repeat = [0]
        next_idx = [0]
        for i in range(1, n1+1):
            idx = 0
            cnt = 0
            for j in range(len(s1)):
                if s1[j] == s2[idx]:
                    idx += 1
                    if idx == len(s2):
                        idx = 0
                        cnt += 1
            repeat.append(repeat[-1] + cnt)
            next_idx.append(idx)
            for j in range(i):
                if next_idx[j] == idx:
                    prefix = repeat[j]
                    pattern = repeat[i] - repeat[j]
                    pattern_len = i - j
                    remain = n1 - j
                    ans = prefix + (remain // pattern_len) * pattern
                    ans += repeat[j + remain % pattern_len] - repeat[j]
                    return ans // n2
        return repeat[n1] // n2

This is standard.

The faulty code's algorithm:
- It filters s1 to chars in s2 (optimization).
- It loops over s2 and uses find to match one s2 at a time, recording copies consumed.
This is also valid and can be fixed. Let's attempt to fully fix it. It may be more elegant? Let's derive correct version.

Algorithm A (s2-find):
Given s1, n1, s2, n2.
Precheck subset.
Filter s1 to chars in s2? Actually if remove chars not in s2, does the subsequence matching behavior remain? For matching s2, chars not in s2 are irrelevant, so yes. But note removing them changes positions and find indices; but we only need counts, not original positions. It is okay because we are matching subsequence in filtered s1 repeated. Since all chars in filtered s1 are in s2, any char not in s2 would be skipped anyway. So we can filter.

Then we want count how many times s2 can be matched in s1 repeated n1.

We can simulate matching s2 greedily in infinite filtered s1. Let `copies` = number of s1 copies consumed so far? More precise:
- `used` = number of s1 copies fully consumed? Let's define `ct` as number of wraps to next copy. Initially 0. `start` = index in current copy to search from. When find char at ptr in current copy, if ptr == -1, we increment ct (move to next copy) and find from 0. But wait if ptr == -1, we also need to consider that we have skipped the rest of current copy and consumed it fully. ct += 1 means we've moved to next copy. Then find in that next copy. If found, total copies "touched" = ct+1. If not found? Precheck subset ensures found in some copy. Good.
- After matching all chars of s2, `copies_used = ct + 1` (number of s1 copies that have been started/consumed up to current position). We append to `rec`.

But there is a subtle bug: If ptr == -1 and we increment ct by 1, then find char in new copy. But what if the char is found at position 0 of new copy; start = ptr+1 =1. Good. If char is not found in new copy? Since char in s2 and s1 contains char, find will find. Good.

Now after matching s2, state for next s2 is `start` (position after last char in current copy) and `ct` (number of completed copies). The total copies consumed so far is `ct+1`. To know if we can match another s2 within n1, we compare `ct+1` to n1. Actually if `ct+1 <= n1`, current s2 is within limit. If `ct+1 > n1`, current s2 is not fully within limit. Let's define rec list where rec[k] = number of s1 copies consumed to match k s2's (for k=0..current). rec[0]=0. After matching k s2's, rec[k] = ct+1. State after k s2's is `ptr` (position of last matched char in current s1 copy), and `start = ptr+1`. The number of copies consumed is rec[k].

If state ptr at current j repeats earlier i, then from i to j, rec increased by cycle1 = rec[j]-rec[i], and s2 count increased by cycle2 = j-i. The process from state i repeats every cycle2 s2 matches and consumes cycle1 s1 copies.

Given total copies n1. We have already consumed rec[i] copies to reach state i. We can repeat the cycle as many times as possible within remaining copies `rest = n1 - rec[i]`. Number of full cycles = rest // cycle1. Each full cycle yields cycle2 s2 matches. After full cycles, remaining copies = rest % cycle1. Starting from state i, we can match additional s2's as long as their rec difference from rec[i] <= remaining copies. Need count additional s2 matches.

Full cycles add `full_cycles * cycle2` s2 matches.

Pseudo:

```
from collections import defaultdict

class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(ch for ch in s1 if ch in set(s2))

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1

            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2
            if rec[-1] == n1:
                return (len(rec) - 1) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        i = track[ptr]
        j = len(rec) - 1
        prefix_copies = rec[i]
        cycle_copies = rec[j] - rec[i]
        cycle_s2 = j - i

        rest_copies = n1 - prefix_copies
        full_cycles = rest_copies // cycle_copies
        rem_copies = rest_copies % cycle_copies

        extra = 0
        while i + extra + 1 <= j and rec[i + extra + 1] - rec[i] <= rem_copies:
            extra += 1

        total_s2 = i + full_cycles * cycle_s2 + extra
        return total_s2 // n2
```

The standard cycle formula for this algorithm:
We have a sequence of states after each s2: state k = (ptr_k, rec_k). rec_k = ct_k+1. If state i and j have same ptr, then the sequence of states from i to j-1 repeats shifted by cycle_copies in rec? Actually state k and state k+cycle_s2 have same ptr, and rec_{k+cycle_s2} = rec_k + cycle_copies. Because the matching process from a given ptr consumes a fixed number of additional copies per s2, independent of absolute ct. Thus for any t >=0, rec_{i + t*cycle_s2 + r} = rec_i + t*cycle_copies + (rec_{i+r} - rec_i), for 0 <= r <= cycle_s2? More generally, the sequence of rec increments per s2 from state i repeats with period cycle_s2, and each period adds cycle_copies to rec. So after q full cycles and r extra s2's, rec = rec_i + q*cycle_copies + (rec_{i+r} - rec_i). We need this <= n1? Wait rec is current copy index+1, not consumed chars. The condition for being able to match those s2's within n1 copies is not simply rec <= n1? Actually if rec <= n1, we are within or before the last copy? If rec <= n1, current copy index <= n1-1, so there are chars available (unless at end of last copy? But if at end of last copy, rec could be n1 and start=len(s1); then no chars available. However, if we are at end of last copy, the next s2 would require rec > n1. The rec value of a completed s2 is the current copy index+1 after matching its last char. If rec <= n1, the last char was matched in a copy index <= n1-1, so it is within available copies. Thus the s2 is fully within n1. If rec > n1, it used a copy beyond last. So condition for a completed s2 to be valid is rec <= n1. Wait earlier s1="aaa", s2="aa", n1=2, third s2 rec=2 <=2, valid. Fourth s2 rec=3 >2, invalid. So yes, a matched s2 is valid iff its rec value <= n1. Therefore, to count maximum valid s2's, we need find largest number of s2's K such that rec_K <= n1, where rec_K is the value in the infinite sequence. The cycle formula should compute the largest K with rec_K <= n1. This is exactly what we need.

So `rest = n1 - rec_i` is the allowed additional rec increase from state i. We can repeat cycles while `cycle_copies <= rest`? Actually each full cycle increases rec by cycle_copies. Number of full cycles = rest // cycle_copies. Then remaining rec budget = rest % cycle_copies. Then extra s2's from state i with rec increase <= remaining budget. This is correct! It doesn't matter that after full cycles we are at a different state; the rec sequence from state i repeats with period cycle_s2 and slope cycle_copies. The condition rec_K <= n1 translates to q*cycle_copies + (rec_{i+r}-rec_i) <= rest. So formula is valid.

Now, let's revisit original code's cycle detection and return formula. We can fix it with:
- `if rec[-1] > n1: return (len(rec)-2)//n2`
- no `>=`
- after cycle:
```
i = track[ptr]
j = len(rec) - 1
prefix = i
cycle_copies = rec[j] - rec[i]
cycle_s2 = j - i
rest = n1 - rec[i]
full = rest // cycle_copies
rem = rest % cycle_copies
extra = 0
while i + extra + 1 <= j and rec[i + extra + 1] - rec[i] <= rem:
    extra += 1
return (prefix + full * cycle_s2 + extra) // n2
```
Need ensure `cycle_copies > 0`; if not, division by zero. Could cycle_copies be 0? As argued no, but let's prove: If rec[j] == rec[i], then ct at j equals ct at i. Same ptr means same start. The state (ct, start) identical. Then from i to j, we matched cycle_s2 >0 s2's and returned to identical state, meaning we consumed a positive number of characters but no additional copies and same position, impossible because s1 finite and each s2 consumes at least one char. So cycle_copies >0. Good.

Now, what about `track` using `ptr` only. If same ptr occurs but different start? start = ptr+1, so same. Good.

Now, need consider if `ptr` is -1? No, after matching each char, ptr is non-negative because precheck and find. Good.

Maybe cycle_s2 always 1 for this algorithm? Let's think. The state is ptr = position of last char of s2 in current copy. Since s2 is fixed, the last char is fixed. The position of that char in s1 is determined by how many copies were skipped. After matching one s2, the next s2 starts after that position. The process of matching the same s2 again from a given start position is a deterministic function f(start) = new_start. The state ptr = start-1. The sequence of start positions is f^k(start0). It could have period >1. Let's find f for a specific s1,s2.

Take s1="abc", s2="abca" (length4). We simulated rec: first rec=2 ptr0, second rec=4 ptr0? Actually for n1=4, second rec=4, ptr0. cycle_s2=1.

Iter2: start3; a -1 ct=2 ptr0 start1; b1 start2; c2 start3; rec=3 ptr2 repeat.

It seems because after matching the last char of s2, the next s2 starts immediately after that char, and the same pattern of relative positions repeats after one s2? Actually f(start) might be start + len(s2) modulo len(s1) adjusted by skips. Since s2 is fixed, the number of skips per s2 is constant, so f is a translation by some amount modulo len(s1). A translation on a cycle has period len(s1)/gcd(shift,len(s1)), which can be >1. But our state ptr = start-1. If f is translation by shift, then period can be >1. Why did examples show period 1? Because shift maybe 0 mod len(s1)? Let's compute shift for s1="abc", s2="abca": start0=0, after match start=1. shift=1. len=3, period 3. But we observed ptr0 repeat after 1? Let's write final fixed code:

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(ch for ch in s1 if ch in set(s2))

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1

            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        i = track[ptr]
        j = len(rec) - 1

        cycle_start = rec[i]
        cycle_copies = rec[j] - rec[i]
        cycle_s2 = j - i

        rest = n1 - cycle_start
        full_cycles = rest // cycle_copies
        rem_copies = rest % cycle_copies

        extra = 0
        while i + extra + 1 <= j and rec[i + extra + 1] - rec[i] <= rem_copies:
            extra += 1

        total_s2 = i + full_cycles * cycle_s2 + extra
        return total_s2 // n2
```

Need verify with original code's `rec` and `track` indexing. `track[ptr] = len(rec)-1` after append. If ptr repeats, `i = track[ptr]` is previous index. `j = len(rec)-1` current. Good.

Potential issue: If `rec[-1] > n1` after appending, we return `(len(rec)-2)//n2`. But what if `len(rec)-2` negative? If n1=0 and first s2 invalid, len=2, return 0. Good. If n1 positive, first s2 valid? Could be invalid if s2 longer than n1 copies; len=2, return 0. Good.

Potential issue: If `n1` is very large and no cycle detected before rec[-1] > n1? Since ptr states finite, cycle will be detected before or at n1? Actually if n1 is small, rec[-1] > n1 may happen before any ptr repeat. If n1 is large, ptr repeat will happen before rec[-1] > n1? Not necessarily; ptr states finite = len(s1). Each s2 increases rec by at least 0, but to exceed n1, need many s2. If n1 is huge, after len(s1)+1 s2's, a ptr repeats. rec at that point may be <= n1 if n1 huge, so cycle detected. If n1 is small, early return. So loop terminates.

Potential issue: `cycle_copies` could be 0? We argued no. But to be safe, if cycle_copies == 0, division by zero. Could add guard? Not needed but maybe safe. Let's see if possible with same ptr and rec same? We argued impossible because same state (ct,start) would repeat after positive matches, meaning infinite matches in zero additional copies. But could rec same but ct different? rec = ct+1, so same rec means same ct. Same ptr means same start. So identical state. Positive matches from identical state would consume characters and end at same state, impossible because s1 finite and no copy advancement? Actually if you match s2 and end at same position in same copy, you must have consumed a positive number of characters and then somehow rewound? No. So impossible. Good.

Potential issue: The `extra` loop uses `rec[i + extra + 1] - rec[i] <= rem_copies`. Since rec can stay same, extra can count multiple. But what if `rem_copies` is large and extra should go beyond j-i? We restrict to `<= j`. Since rem_copies < cycle_copies, and one full cycle increases rec by cycle_copies, the number of extra s2's with rec increase <= rem_copies cannot exceed cycle_s2? Actually if rem_copies < cycle_copies, could extra exceed cycle_s2? Suppose rec increases by 0 for many s2, then by cycle_copies at the end. If rem_copies < cycle_copies, extra could be up to cycle_s2-1 because the last s2 in the cycle causes rec increase to cycle_copies > rem_copies. So within j. Good.

Potential issue: If `full_cycles` computed as `rest // cycle_copies`, and `rest` is n1 - rec[i]. Since rec[i] is current copy index+1, not consumed chars. We already validated with examples. Need be absolutely sure. Let's prove.

We have infinite sequence rec_k for k=0,1,2,... where rec_k = number of copies touched after matching k s2's (ct+1). A matched s2 is valid within n1 copies iff rec_k <= n1? Wait if rec_k == n1 and start_k == len(s1), the s2 is valid (it ended exactly at end of last copy). rec_k <= n1. If rec_k == n1 and start_k < len(s1), valid. If rec_k > n1, invalid. So condition rec_k <= n1 is necessary and sufficient for the k-th s2 to be fully within n1 copies. Is it sufficient? Suppose rec_k = n1 but the last char was matched in copy n1-1 at some position, yes within. If rec_k = n1 and start_k maybe > len(s1)? start is always <= len(s1) after find? If ptr = len(s1)-1, start=len(s1). If ptr found at len(s1)-1, start=len(s1). So start <= len(s1). Good. So rec_k <= n1 means current copy index = rec_k-1 <= n1-1, so last char in available copies. Thus valid.

Now, the sequence rec_k has the property that for k >= i, rec_{k+cycle_s2} = rec_k + cycle_copies? Because state ptr repeats every cycle_s2, so the number of copies touched after one cycle increases by cycle_copies. More generally, for any q and r (0<=r<cycle_s2), rec_{i+q*cycle_s2+r} = rec_i + q*cycle_copies + (rec_{i+r} - rec_i). This is true because the matching process from state i+r to i+r+cycle_s2 consumes cycle_copies copies and returns to same ptr, so rec_{i+r+cycle_s2} = rec_{i+r} + cycle_copies. By induction. Thus the set of rec values for all future s2's is exactly rec_i + q*cycle_copies + delta_r, where delta_r = rec_{i+r} - rec_i for r=0..cycle_s2-1. We need count pairs (q,r) such that q*cycle_copies + delta_r <= rest = n1 - rec_i. The maximum q is rest // cycle_copies, but if delta_r can be 0 for some r, then for q = full_cycles, r with delta_r <= rem_copies also valid. Our formula counts prefix i, full_cycles*cycle_s2, plus extra r satisfying delta_r <= rem_copies. This is correct. Note: What about q = full_cycles and r with delta_r <= rem_copies; yes. What about q < full_cycles, all r valid because q*cycle_copies + delta_r <= (full_cycles-1)*cycle_copies + max_delta. max_delta < cycle_copies? Is delta_r < cycle_copies for r < cycle_s2? Since delta_{cycle_s2} = cycle_copies, and rec is nondecreasing, delta_r <= cycle_copies. Could delta_r equal cycle_copies for r < cycle_s2? That would mean rec_{i+r} - rec_i = cycle_copies before completing the cycle, so rec_{i+r} = rec_{i+cycle_s2}. Since rec nondecreasing and state at i+r maybe different, could happen? If rec reaches cycle_copies early, then the remaining s2's in the cycle have delta = cycle_copies (no additional copies). That would mean cycle_copies copies are consumed in the first part, then zero copies for rest. Is that possible? Yes, e.g., s1="aaa", s2="a", cycle_s2=3, cycle_copies=1, deltas: rec2-rec1=0, rec3-rec1=0, rec4-rec1=1. max delta=1=cycle_copies at r=3 (cycle_s2). For r<cycle_s2, delta < cycle_copies. Could delta_r = cycle_copies for r<cycle_s2? That would mean after r s2's, rec already increased by cycle_copies, and the remaining cycle_s2-r s2's consume zero additional copies, ending at same rec. Then rec_{i+r} = rec_{i+cycle_s2} and ptr at i+r maybe? If rec same and ptr same? Not necessarily ptr different. But if rec same as cycle end, and after remaining s2's rec stays same, then those remaining s2's consume zero copies. Possible? Example maybe. If delta_r = cycle_copies for r<cycle_s2, then for q = full_cycles, r with delta_r <= rem_copies (rem < cycle_copies) would not include r because delta_r = cycle_copies > rem. So okay. For q < full_cycles, q*cycle_copies + delta_r <= (full_cycles-1)*cycle_copies + cycle_copies = full_cycles*cycle_copies <= rest, so valid. So formula counts all. Good.

Now, one more issue: `extra` loop starts at r=1 and checks `rec[i+r] - rec[i] <= rem_copies`. It counts r values. But if `rem_copies` is large enough to include delta_r = cycle_copies? Since rem_copies < cycle_copies, no. So extra <= cycle_s2-1. Good.

Let's produce final answer:

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in set(s2))

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1

            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        i = track[ptr]
        j = len(rec) - 1

        cycle_copies = rec[j] - rec[i]
        cycle_s2 = j - i

        rest = n1 - rec[i]
        full_cycles = rest // cycle_copies
        rem_copies = rest % cycle_copies

        extra = 0
        while i + extra + 1 <= j and rec[i + extra + 1] - rec[i] <= rem_copies:
            extra += 1

        return (i + full_cycles * cycle_s2 + extra) // n2
```

Need check if `cycle_copies` could be 0? We believe no. But to be safe, maybe if `cycle_copies == 0`, return something? Not needed. But adding a guard might hide bugs. No.

Let's write final code elegantly:

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s2_set = set(s2)
        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in s2_set)

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1

            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        i = track[ptr]
        j = len(rec) - 1
        cycle_copies = rec[j] - rec[i]
        cycle_s2 = j - i

        rest = n1 - rec[i]
        full_cycles = rest // cycle_copies
        rem_copies = rest % cycle_copies

        extra = 0
        while i + extra + 1 <= j and rec[i + extra + 1] - rec[i] <= rem_copies:
            extra += 1

        return (i + full_cycles * cycle_s2 + extra) // n2
```

Maybe for any s1,s2, the state after matching s2 is always the same? That can't be. Let's think: The state is the position of the last char of s2 in the current copy. Since s2 is fixed, the last char is fixed. The position of that char in s1 is determined by how many copies were skipped. The number of copies skipped while matching s2 depends on the starting position. If starting position changes, the number of skips may change, but the ending position might be the same due to periodicity? Not always. Let's try s1="ab", s2="ba": start0=0 -> end ptr0. start1=1 -> end ptr0. Same. s1="abc", s2="ca": start0=0 -> end ptr0. start1=1 -> end ptr0. It seems end ptr is always the position of the last char in s1? For s2="ca", last char a, position of a in s1 is 0. End ptr=0. For s2="acb", last char b, position of b in s1 is 1. End ptr=1. For s2="abca", last char a, position of a in s1 is 0. End ptr=0. Indeed, the last char of s2 is a fixed char c. When matching c, `s1.find(c)` from some start either finds it in current copy at its first occurrence after start, or wraps and finds its first occurrence in next copy. The position of that first occurrence is fixed: it's the first occurrence of c in s1. Since `s1.find(c)` from 0 returns the first occurrence. Now, let's ensure final code doesn't have syntax errors. Let's read:

```
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        if not set(s2).issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s2_set = set(s2)
        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in s2_set)

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1

            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        i = track[ptr]
        j = len(rec) - 1

        cycle_copies = rec[j] - rec[i]
        cycle_s2 = j - i

        rest = n1 - rec[i]
        full_cycles = rest // cycle_copies
        rem_copies = rest % cycle_copies

        extra = 0
        while i + extra + 1 <= j and rec[i + extra + 1] - rec[i] <= rem_copies:
            extra += 1

        return (i + full_cycles * cycle_s2 + extra) // n2
```

Looks good.

i+1=2 rec[2]-rec[1]=1 <=0 false. total=1+2=3. Good.

Wait, if a char is in the next copy, in original it is after the end of current copy. In filtered s1, the next copy is also after the end of current filtered copy. Removing non-matching chars from the end of current copy does not move the next copy's chars into current copy; it just shortens the current copy.
</think>

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```python
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        s2_set = set(s2)
        if not s2_set.issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in s2_set)

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1

            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        i = track[ptr]
        j = len(rec) - 1

        cycle_copies = rec[j] - rec[i]
        cycle_s2 = j - i

        rest = n1 - rec[i]
        full_cycles = rest // cycle_copies
        rem_copies = rest % cycle_copies

        extra = 0
        while i + extra + 1 <= j and rec[i + extra + 1] - rec[i] <= rem_copies:
            extra += 1

        return (i + full_cycles * cycle_s2 + extra) // n2
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
class Solution:
    def getMaxRepetitions(self, s1: str, n1: int, s2: str, n2: int) -> int:
        s2_set = set(s2)
        if not s2_set.issubset(set(s1)):
            return 0

        s1 = ''.join(c for c in s1 if c in s2_set)

        rec = [0]
        track = {}
        ct = 0
        start = 0

        while True:
            for ch in s2:
                ptr = s1.find(ch, start)
                if ptr == -1:
                    ct += 1
                    ptr = s1.find(ch)
                start = ptr + 1

            rec.append(ct + 1)

            if rec[-1] > n1:
                return (len(rec) - 2) // n2

            if ptr not in track:
                track[ptr] = len(rec) - 1
            else:
                break

        i = track[ptr]
        j = len(rec) - 1

        cycle_copies = rec[j] - rec[i]
        cycle_s2 = j - i

        rest = n1 - rec[i]
        full_cycles = rest // cycle_copies
        rem_copies = rest % cycle_copies

        extra = 0
        while i + extra + 1 <= j and rec[i + extra + 1] - rec[i] <= rem_copies:
            extra += 1

        return (i + full_cycles * cycle_s2 + extra) // n2
